People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um... well, I hope I'm in the right place, but I came in because I've just been feeling a bit... well, honestly, just worn out all the time. 

I know everyone gets tired, and I don't want to make a huge deal out of nothing, but it's just that even after a full night in bed, my body feels like it's made of sandbags. Does that make sense? I'm still getting my work done—I do market research, so I really try to just push through it—but getting through a workday, or even just doing something simple like folding the laundry at home... it just takes absolutely everything I have right now. 

I'm sorry to complain, but... is that the kind of thing I'm supposed to talk about here?

Um, well... I don't want to sound dramatic, and I really do try to stay positive. But, I guess, yes, maybe a little down? 

It's not always crying or anything like that. It's more just... most days I wake up with this really heavy, kind of flat feeling right in my chest. And sometimes I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, staring out at the yard, and I just get this overwhelming sense that... well, that there's no good version of the future coming. 

I try really hard to shake it off and just get to work, I promise I do. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly pessimistic or ungrateful for what I have. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say? Is it okay to feel like that sometimes?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really try to keep up with my hobbies, I do. I know it's important to stay active. But... I guess lately, things just haven't been quite as appealing? 

I used to always make time for things, you know? Like going for hikes, or doing yoga, working out in my garden... I even loved taking photos of birds. But recently, I just... I keep canceling plans. Or I'll look at my camera sitting in the closet and I just think... well, I just can't seem to find the energy to take it out. It just doesn't feel worth the effort right now, if that makes sense. 

I feel terrible admitting that, because I know I should be doing things that are good for me. I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up on everything. Is it normal to just... lose the spark for things like that when you're feeling this tired?

Um... well, no, not... not exactly suicide. I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself, I promise. I know how terrible that would be for my family.

I'm still getting my work done—I do market research, so I really try to just push through it—but getting through a workday, or even just doing something simple like folding the laundry at home... it just takes absolutely everything I have right now. \n\nI'm sorry to complain, but... is that the kind of thing I'm supposed to talk about here?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't want to sound dramatic, and I really do try to stay positive. But, I guess, yes, maybe a little down? \n\nIt's not always crying or anything like that. It's more just... most days I wake up with this really heavy, kind of flat feeling right in my chest. And sometimes I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, staring out at the yard, and I just get this overwhelming sense that... well, that there's no good version of the future coming. \n\nI try really hard to shake it off and just get to work, I promise I do. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly pessimistic or ungrateful for what I have. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say? Is it okay to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat feeling in your chest?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess the really exhausted, slowed-down part—that heavy feeling—it's been happening most days for... oh, maybe about the past two months? \n\nI kept thinking it would just pass if I tried a bit harder to stay upbeat and get enough sleep, you know? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... a burden? No, I wouldn't say I feel like a burden to anyone. My husband is so supportive, and I really try my best to pull my weight at home. I don't want to be a drag on anyone, you know?

But... I guess... well, lately I have been feeling a little bad about some things. Like, we recently made a decision about our mortgage, and... I don't know, I just keep replaying it in my head. I keep telling myself I was being selfish, or reckless, and that I put our whole household at risk. 

My husband keeps reminding me that we made the decision together, and that it's fine, but... I just can't shake this feeling that I messed everything up. It's silly, I know. I'm probably just overthinking it, right? I mean, everyone second-guesses big financial decisions sometimes. That's completely normal, isn't it? I just want to be a good partner and not cause any problems.

Um... well, it's kind of like I was saying before... I really don't want you to think I'm depressed or anything. I mean, I used to always make time for my hiking and yoga, and taking care of the garden, and taking my bird photos... I love those things!

Um... well, if I really sit down and think about it... I guess this whole... you know, feeling so exhausted and just completely slowed down... it's been happening most days for about the past two months.

Is it... is it normal for a rut like this to last a couple of months? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I can get back on track. I promise I'm usually a much more upbeat person!

Um... worthless? Oh gosh, I wouldn't use that word. That sounds so... so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want you to think I have some terrible self-esteem issue or anything like that. I'm usually very confident in my work!

But... I guess... well, I have been feeling a little bit bad about some things lately. Just... normal things, I think? Like, we recently refinanced our mortgage, and... well, I pushed for it because I thought it was the right move at the time. But lately, I just keep replaying it over and over in my head. I keep telling myself I was being so selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. 

My husband is so sweet, he keeps reminding me that we looked at the numbers and made the decision together, and that we're fine. But I just can't shake this awful feeling that I messed everything up. It's just me being a little overly responsible, right? I'm sure it's totally normal to second-guess financial stuff like that... isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess it started right after we finalized everything with the mortgage... which was about two months ago. 

It's kind of funny, because right before we signed the papers, I was in this... this really overcharged kind of business push, you know? Just getting so much done, feeling super driven with all my research reports and the house stuff. Which is a good thing, right? I mean, everyone likes being productive! 

But then, almost as soon as it was all a done deal, my mood just sort of... swung right into this low stretch I've been telling you about. I really hope that doesn't sound too strange or all over the place. It's probably completely normal to have a bit of an emotional crash after pushing so hard for a while, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay and not making it sound weirder than it is.

I haven't made any kind of plan or anything like that, I swear. \n\nIt's just that the thought keeps coming back, and... well, it does scare me a little bit when it happens. Like I said, I've been trying really hard to keep my eating habits on track. Um... interfering? I really wouldn't want to say it's *interfering*. That makes it sound like I'm completely falling apart, and I promise I'm not! I'm still showing up, you know? 

I mean... I guess things have been a little bit harder lately. I might be... just a tiny bit behind on a couple of my client reports. But I'm catching up! I really am trying my best to stay on top of things. It just takes so much energy to get through the workday and handle the basic stuff around the house right now. 

And... well, I haven't been seeing my friends as much, or doing my usual outdoor walks. I just haven't had it in me. But I'm sure it's just a phase, right? Everyone gets a little overwhelmed and needs to pull back sometimes to focus on work and home. That's a responsible way to handle it, don't you think? I just want to make sure you know I'm still trying really hard to keep everything together.

I'm still getting my work done—I do market research, so I really try to just push through it—but getting through a workday, or even just doing something simple like folding the laundry at home... it just takes absolutely everything I have right now. \n\nI'm sorry to complain, but... is that the kind of thing I'm supposed to talk about here?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't want to sound dramatic, and I really do try to stay positive. But, I guess, yes, maybe a little down? \n\nIt's not always crying or anything like that. It's more just... most days I wake up with this really heavy, kind of flat feeling right in my chest. And sometimes I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, staring out at the yard, and I just get this overwhelming sense that... well, that there's no good version of the future coming. \n\nI try really hard to shake it off and just get to work, I promise I do. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly pessimistic or ungrateful for what I have. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say? Is it okay to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat feeling in your chest?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess the really exhausted, slowed-down part—that heavy feeling—it's been happening most days for... oh, maybe about the past two months? \n\nI kept thinking it would just pass if I tried a bit harder to stay upbeat and get enough sleep, you know? I really didn't want to bother anyone with it if it was just a normal slump. Two months probably sounds like a long time to just let it go on before getting help, I'm sorry. I hope it's okay that I waited, I just... I really wanted to fix it myself and not be a burden. Is it normal for something like this to last that long?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with my hobbies, I do. I know it's important to stay active. But... I guess lately, things just haven't been quite as appealing? \n\nI used to always make time for things, you know? Like going for hikes, or doing yoga, working out in my garden... I even loved taking photos of birds. But recently, I just... I keep canceling plans. Or I'll look at my camera sitting in the closet and I just think... well, I just can't seem to find the energy to take it out. It just doesn't feel worth the effort right now, if that makes sense. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, because I know I should be doing things that are good for me. I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up on everything. Is it normal to just... lose the spark for things like that when you're feeling this tired?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Um... my appetite? Well, I think it's been pretty normal, really. I mean, I try to make sure I'm eating enough and getting decent meals in. I don't want to neglect my health or anything like that. \n\nI guess I haven't really noticed any big changes. Maybe I'm just eating what I normally do? I hope that's the right answer. I just haven't paid super close attention to it, to be honest. Is it bad if my appetite hasn't changed? I know sometimes people eat more or less when they feel down, but I think I've just been keeping on track with it. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not... not exactly suicide. I mean, I would never actually do anything to hurt myself, I promise. I know how terrible that would be for my family. \n\nBut... I guess sometimes, especially late at night when everything is quiet and I can't sleep, my mind just sort of wanders to some dark places? I'll catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe my husband would just be better off without me dragging him down. It's just a passing thought, really! I haven't made any kind of plan or anything like that, I swear. \n\nIt's just that the thought keeps coming back, and... well, it does scare me a little bit when it happens. I try to push it away as soon as it pops up. I hope you don't think I'm crazy or a danger to myself for having thoughts like that. Is it common to have those kinds of thoughts when you're in a slump like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not that I've noticed. Like I said, I've been trying really hard to keep my eating habits on track. I know how important it is to take care of myself, especially when I'm not feeling my best. \n\nI haven't been weighing myself or anything, but my clothes all seem to fit exactly the same. So I don't think my weight has fluctuated at all. I really hope that's a good sign? It means I'm at least doing something right, doesn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of pleasure in almost all activities for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess... well, I wouldn't say *all* activities. I really try to stay busy and keep up with my hobbies. I usually love hiking, and yoga, and taking care of my garden. Oh, and bird photography! I have a really nice camera for it. \n\nBut... I suppose lately, I have been, um, maybe canceling a few things? Or... letting my camera just sit in the closet. It's not that I don't like those things anymore! I just... I don't know, it just hasn't felt like it's worth the effort to get everything set up and go out. \n\nI'm sure I'll get back to it soon, though! It's probably just a little phase, right? I mean, everyone gets a little tired sometimes and wants to just stay in. That's normal, isn't it? I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling like activities you usually enjoy aren't worth the effort?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it hasn't been that long, I don't think. I mean, I'm really not entirely sure? The days just sort of blend together lately. \n\nMaybe... maybe a few weeks? Or a month? It's really hard to pinpoint exactly when I stopped wanting to take my camera out. It just kind of happened gradually. I promise I haven't been like this forever, though! I'm usually very active and involved in my hobbies. \n\nI'm sorry I can't give you a more exact timeframe. Is it okay if I'm not totally sure? I really am trying to remember, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're a burden to others most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um... a burden? No, I wouldn't say I feel like a burden to anyone. My husband is so supportive, and I really try my best to pull my weight at home. I don't want to be a drag on anyone, you know?\n\nBut... I guess... well, lately I have been feeling a little bad about some things. Like, we recently made a decision about our mortgage, and... I don't know, I just keep replaying it in my head. I keep telling myself I was being selfish, or reckless, and that I put our whole household at risk. \n\nMy husband keeps reminding me that we made the decision together, and that it's fine, but... I just can't shake this feeling that I messed everything up. It's silly, I know. I'm probably just overthinking it, right? I mean, everyone second-guesses big financial decisions sometimes. That's completely normal, isn't it? I just want to be a good partner and not cause any problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the time, even when doing things that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of like I was saying before... I really don't want you to think I'm depressed or anything. I mean, I used to always make time for my hiking and yoga, and taking care of the garden, and taking my bird photos... I love those things! \n\nBut... I guess lately, I just... I keep canceling on my hiking group. And my camera is just sitting in the closet. It's not that I don't want to enjoy them! It's just... I don't know, none of it really feels worth the effort right now? Like, I try, but the good feeling just isn't there. \n\nI'm sure I'll snap out of it soon, though. It's probably just a little burnout from work, right? That makes sense, doesn't it? I really am trying to push through it.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling like you can't experience pleasure from things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, if I really sit down and think about it... I guess this whole... you know, feeling so exhausted and just completely slowed down... it's been happening most days for about the past two months. \n\nI really hate saying that out loud. Two months sounds like such a long time to just be in a slump, doesn't it? I really hope you don't think I'm just being lazy or giving up. I've been trying so hard to keep a smile on and push through it at work, I really have. \n\nIs it... is it normal for a rut like this to last a couple of months? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so I can get back on track. I promise I'm usually a much more upbeat person!\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of exhaustion and lack of pleasure for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... most of the day? I... I don't know if I would say it's *most* of the day. I really don't want to exaggerate and make it sound worse than it actually is. \n\nI mean, I still get up and log in for work, and I do my absolute best to get my market research reports done on time. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing from morning till night! I guess I haven't really paid attention to exactly how many hours it lasts... maybe it comes and goes? \n\nI just know it's been kind of hanging over me lately. But I definitely try to push through and have good moments during the day, you know? I'm really trying my hardest to keep things as normal as possible. That's the best way to handle it, right? I just don't want to be making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty most days lately?\n\nPatient: Um... worthless? Oh gosh, I wouldn't use that word. That sounds so... so dramatic, doesn't it? I really don't want you to think I have some terrible self-esteem issue or anything like that. I'm usually very confident in my work!\n\nBut... I guess... well, I have been feeling a little bit bad about some things lately. Just... normal things, I think? Like, we recently refinanced our mortgage, and... well, I pushed for it because I thought it was the right move at the time. But lately, I just keep replaying it over and over in my head. I keep telling myself I was being so selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. \n\nMy husband is so sweet, he keeps reminding me that we looked at the numbers and made the decision together, and that we're fine. But I just can't shake this awful feeling that I messed everything up. It's just me being a little overly responsible, right? I'm sure it's totally normal to second-guess financial stuff like that... isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling bad about the mortgage refinance decision?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it started right after we finalized everything with the mortgage... which was about two months ago. \n\nIt's kind of funny, because right before we signed the papers, I was in this... this really overcharged kind of business push, you know? Just getting so much done, feeling super driven with all my research reports and the house stuff. Which is a good thing, right? I mean, everyone likes being productive! \n\nBut then, almost as soon as it was all a done deal, my mood just sort of... swung right into this low stretch I've been telling you about. I really hope that doesn't sound too strange or all over the place. It's probably completely normal to have a bit of an emotional crash after pushing so hard for a while, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay and not making it sound weirder than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and exhaustion are interfering with your ability to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Um... interfering? I really wouldn't want to say it's *interfering*. That makes it sound like I'm completely falling apart, and I promise I'm not! I'm still showing up, you know? \n\nI mean... I guess things have been a little bit harder lately. I might be... just a tiny bit behind on a couple of my client reports. But I'm catching up! I really am trying my best to stay on top of things. It just takes so much energy to get through the workday and handle the basic stuff around the house right now. \n\nAnd... well, I haven't been seeing my friends as much, or doing my usual outdoor walks. I just haven't had it in me. But I'm sure it's just a phase, right? Everyone gets a little overwhelmed and needs to pull back sometimes to focus on work and home. That's a responsible way to handle it, don't you think? I just want to make sure you know I'm still trying really hard to keep everything together.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in doing?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I still *like* doing things! I really do. I don't want you to think I've just given up or anything like that, I promise. \n\nI usually love making time for... well, hiking, and my yoga classes. And I have a garden I normally take a lot of pride in, and I love taking bird photos. I have this really wonderful camera. \n\nBut... I guess lately, I just keep canceling my plans. And my camera is just sort of... sitting in the closet right now. It just hasn't really felt worth the effort to get it all out and do it, if that makes sense? But I figure that's just me being practical and saving my energy for my job and my husband, right? It's totally okay to put hobbies on the back burner for a little bit when you're feeling drained, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm prioritizing the right things.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling guilty about the mortgage refinance decision?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been the whole two months, really, ever since we signed the papers. I just... I hope I don't sound overly dramatic saying this, because I know we're okay. I really do. \n\nBut I just keep replaying the whole decision in my head over and over. I keep telling myself that I was being so selfish and... and just reckless, you know? Like I somehow put our whole household at risk by pushing for it. My husband is so sweet, he keeps reminding me that we made the decision together and that we're completely fine. And I know he's right! I really do listen to him, and I appreciate him so much. \n\nIt's just hard to turn that little guilty voice off sometimes. But it's totally normal to second-guess big financial things and feel a little bad about it, right? I'm sure everyone worries about being a responsible partner when it comes to money. I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting or making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Um... well, this whole feeling of just being so slowed down and, you know, exhausted and not wanting to do my hobbies... I guess it's been going on for most days for about the past two months.
